# finresearch

Lightweight prototype of a financial-research agent.

Everything is processed at run time. The corpus is loaded from JSONL into memory. There is no vector store, embedding model, or persisted index. A LangChain agent runs the selected skill's tool plan. DSPy modules handle classification and prompting. Every model call goes through OpenRouter. Pydantic types every boundary, and outputs are saved as JSON artifacts.

## Layout

```
data/corpus/          synthetic desk emails (day N/emails.jsonl)
data/skills/          runtime research skills (SKILL.md)
data/artifacts/       JSON outputs written at run time (not committed)
src/finresearch/      settings, models, OpenRouter clients, LangChain agent, DSPy modules, artifacts
scripts/              synthetic corpus generation
docs/                 corpus documentation and AI transcripts
```

## Setup

```bash
uv sync --all-groups
cp .env.example .env   # then set OPENROUTER_API_KEY
```

You need an OpenRouter API key. There is no offline mock. `AGENT_MODEL` picks the model for the LangChain agent and `DSPY_MODEL` picks the model for the DSPy modules. Both take OpenRouter model slugs.

## Explore

```bash
uv run finresearch skills list
uv run finresearch skills show risk-factor-scan
uv run finresearch ask "Which litigation risks are disclosed?"
uv run finresearch ask "How do peer margins compare to Harbor Metals?" --skill peer-comparison
uv run pytest
uv run mypy src
```

## How a skill runs

# Generation prompts for the email triage prototype

Six prompts that generate the synthetic and instructional files the build spec calls for. The email corpus has its own prompt, `synthetic_data_prompt_v3.md`, and is not covered here.

Every output is a draft. Read it before the pipeline is tuned on it.

3	Oct 8, 2026 · @Kelvin

5	This is a build spec to be used by a coding agent. The prototype classifies each of 300 synthetic emails by how much attention it needs, then compares the relevant ones with a five-company book and suggests changes for an investment analyst to decide on.
6	
7	## Overview
8	
9	The design is settled and nothing is built yet. The hand-off is this spec, the corpus prompt and six generation prompts. The corpus, the seed files, the criteria files and all code are still to be produced.
10	
11	### Method
12	
13	&#91;embedded content: proposed method · corpus and book in, brief and evaluation out\]
14	
15	Jev reads every email against the desk's relevance criteria and never sees the book. Two pathways follow the gate: attention notes for emails that need a person, and the analysis agent for emails that bear on a thesis. The analyst's decision is the only path by which the book changes.
16	The purpose of this is to automatically triage incoming emails for relevent research insights, identify those emails, and then parse research insights from the to be presented to an analyst.
17

987	## Corpus: 300 test emails and 150 for tuning
988	
989	The corpus is whatever the corpus prompt in `synthetic_data_prompt_v3.md` produces. That prompt is the specification for the emails, and the pipeline adapts to its output. [2026-10-09T03:50:40] ASSISTANT
I've updated the spec to match the repo and added token and latency monitoring. The leftovers from the earlier "finresearch" prototype are archived in `legacy/finresearch/`, not deleted: README, pyproject, uv.lock, .env.example, main.py, `data/README.md` and `data/skills/`. No code has been written yet.

**Corpus folders.** The spec now uses `day_1`, `day_2` and `tuning` as the data set names everywhere: in the `--set` flag, in the output folders (`data/out/<set>/`) and in `EvalReport.corpus`. Each day is a separate run with its own brief and eval report, which also keeps the repeat check within one day. The corpus section now describes the files as they actually are: 14 fields, with no `received_at` (the loader assigns one in file order) and no `redundant_of`. The trading dates are also filled in.

**Tuning set.** The spec now says it is generated outside the build into `data/corpus/tuning/`, and the agent never generates or edits it. Until it exists, tuning and the criteria check are reported as pending and the pipeline runs at starting values.

**Repeat detection.**
- The thresholds are now fixed values instead of being fitted on the tuning set.
- The eval metrics `repeat_recall` and `repeat_match` are gone. It now reports `repeat_flagged` (how many emails the check flags) and `repeat_precision` (the share of flagged emails the corpus labels `redundant`).
- Build step 2 now passes when a report lists every flagged email on `day_1` with its nearest earlier email and both scores.

**Corpus prompt and keys.** All references now point to `docs/corpus_documentation/synthetic_data_prompt.md`, in SPEC.md, KICKOFF.md and `prompts/README.md`. The keys match `.env`: `TYPESAFE_API_KEY` for Jev and `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` for every Claude model. `OPENAI_API_KEY` and `OPENROUTER_API_KEY` are listed as present but unused. I removed the "if the keys are missing" fallbacks.

**Monitoring.** There's a new section, "Monitoring: token use and latency":
- A new `monitoring.py` wraps every model client and the embedder. Each call records its stage, email, model, input and output tokens, whether it hit the cache, and how long it took.
- `run.py` times each stage per email and for the whole set.
- Each run writes `usage.json` and `metrics.json`. The report gives typical (p50), slow (p95) and worst-case latency, and per stage and per model it shows the tokens actually spent next to what the run would have cost without the cache.
- When a provider doesn't report token counts, which Jev may not, they are estimated and marked as estimates.
- The records hold IDs, counts and times only, never prompt or email text.
- A new `/monitor` page shows the results, and the Live page shows tokens and latency for each stage.
- The new data models are added to the spec's contracts, and build steps 0 and 6 now include tests for this.

CLAUDE.md is updated to match all of this.

**Decisions for you:**
- **Tuning set size:** the spec previously said 150 tuning emails split 100/50, but the plan is 100 emails. I changed the split to two-thirds fit, one-third validate.
- **Subject threshold:** the subject-match value was 0.7 in one place and 0.6 in the "Starting values" table. I made both 0.6.
- **Leftover settings:** `.env` also has `LLM_*` and `EMBEDDING_*` settings (for example `LLM_PROVIDER=mock`, `EMBEDDING_PROVIDER=hash`) that the spec doesn't use. I left `.env` alone.
- **Python version:** `.python-version` still says 3.14, but the spec says 3.12.

Next would be build step 0: writing a new `pyproject.toml`, README and `.env.example`, then the data models, config, monitoring, compute/fold and stage stubs. Should I go ahead?

"""Ergonomic answer objects and response metadata, built on the generated wire schemas.

The wire models in `typesafe_sdk._schemas.models` mirror the OpenAPI schema. The public answer types
subclass them, adding immutability and integer-keyed score maps. Because the answer models are tagged,
the public `Answer` union validates by discriminator; per-answer dispatch is kept only to skip answer
kinds a future API adds without failing the whole response.
"""

from functools import cached_property
from typing import Annotated, Any, Literal, TypeAlias

import httpx2
from pydantic import ConfigDict, Field, ValidationError
from typing_extensions import Self, override

from typesafe_sdk._core.json import deserialize, serialize
from typesafe_sdk._core.logging import logger

"""Decision values shared by System One predictors and generative LMs."""

import json
from functools import lru_cache
from typing import Annotated, ClassVar, Literal, get_args, get_origin

from pydantic import ConfigDict, Field, JsonValue, TypeAdapter, create_model, model_serializer
from pydantic.json_schema import SkipJsonSchema
from pydantic_core import core_schema

from dspy.adapters.types.base_type import Type
from dspy.utils.annotation import experimental

Probability = Annotated[float, Field(ge=0, le=1)]
_JSON_ADAPTER = TypeAdapter(JsonValue)


class _Decision(Type):
    model_config = ConfigDict(extra="forbid")
    _criteria_json: ClassVar[str] = "null"

    confidence: Probability = Field(description="Confidence in the value, from 0 to 1.")

    @classmethod
    def criteria(cls):
        """Return an independent copy of the type's declared criteria."""
        return json.loads(cls._criteria_json)

    @model_serializer(mode="wrap")
    def serialize_model(self, handler):
        # Unlike media types, decisions are ordinary structured JSON, including
        # provider evidence when present. Missing evidence is not shown as null.
        return {key: value for key, value in handler(self).items() if value is not None or key == "value"}


@experimental

    or both outcome descriptions. ``A

class TypeSafe:
    """Call TypeSafe's System One API with DSPy's shared request cache.

    Install ``dspy[typesafe]`` to use this client. Model, API key, and endpoint
    default to TYPESAFE_DEFAULT_MODEL, TYPESAFE_API_KEY, and TYPESAFE_BASE_URL.
    Configure with ``dspy.configure(lm=dspy.experimental.TypeSafe(...))`` or pass
    ``lm=`` when calling a predictor. Predict selects System One translation automatically.

    Args:
        model: Model name (defaults to ``jev-latest``).
        api_key: [REDACTED] omitted from saved state and history.
        base_url: Endpoint (defaults to ``https://api.typesafe.ai``).
        cache: Whether to use DSPy's cache.
        timeout: Per-operation timeout in seconds.
    """

    supports_decision_requests = True

    def __init__(
        self,
        model: str | None = None,
        *,
        api_key: str | None = None,
        base_url: str | None = None,
        cache: bool = True,
        timeout: float = 10.0,
        callbacks=None,
    ):

        self.callbacks = list(callbacks or [])
        self.base_url = (base_url or os.getenv("TYPESAFE_BASE_URL") or "https://api.typesafe.ai").rstrip("/")
        self.api_key = api_key
        self.cache = cache
        self.timeout = timeout
        self.history = []

    def _request(self, state, questions):
        return {
            "provider": "typesafe",
            "model": self.model,
            "base_url": self.base_url,
            "state": state,
            "questions": questions,
        }

    def _finish(self, request, response, cache_hit):
        if self.cache and not cache_hit:
            dspy.cache.put(request, response)
        usage = {} if cache_hit else response["usage"]
        if settings.usage_tracker and usage:
            settings.usage_tracker.add_usage(response["model"], usage)
        if not settings.disable_history:
            record_history(
                self,
                {
                    "request": copy.deepcopy(request),
                    "response": copy.deepcopy(response),
                    "usage": usage,
                    "cache_hit": cache_hit,
                    "messages": None,
                    "prompt": json.dumps({"state": request["state"], "questions": request["questions"]}),
                    "outputs": [json.dumps(response["answers"])],
                }
            )
            del self.history[: -settings.max_history_size]
        return copy.deepcopy(response["answers"])

    def _sdk_kwargs(self):
        return {"model": self.model, "api_key": self.api_key, "base_url": self.base_url, "timeout": self.timeout}

  **Choices for DECISIONS.md:**
  1. A review row counts only once every check that applies to its kind is filled; a review key is the passing share of those rows, and stays empty until one is. The checks are:
     - notes: summary_accurate, reason_stated, action_stated
     - existing_thesis: right_pillar, right_stance, sections_support
     - new_thesis: new_to_book, sections_support

     - Quarantined emails (either safety probability ≥ 0.40) are left out of every sweep.
     - Truncated emails do not constrain the pass threshold.
     - The pass threshold is floored to 4 decimal places.
     - F1 candidates are the lowest probability and the midpoints between neighbouring probabilities; ties go to the value nearest the start. A label with no positive example keeps its starting value.

     - It keeps one history entry per criteria version; rerunning a version replaces its entry.
     - Each version's labels go to `data/out/tuning/criteria_check/<version>.json`, so the next version can list label changes. The history contract has no per-email labels.

@pytest.mark.parametrize("question", ["instructs_ai", "possible_mnpi"])
def test_quarantine_on_either_safety_question(question: str) -> None:
    t = triage(triage_probs=probs(0.7, 0.2), safety={"possible_mnpi": 0.0, "instructs_ai": 0.0, question: 0.4},
               ticker_probs={**{x: 0.0 for x in config.TICKERS}, "NVDA": 0.9}, attention=0.9,
               topic_probs={**{x: 0.0 for x in config.TOPICS}, "macro": 0.9}, truncated=True)
    r = gate.process(t, REPEAT, T)
    assert (r.triage, r.decided_by, r.gate, r.redundant_of) == (None, "quarantine", "quarantine", None)
    assert r.affected_tickers == [] and r.additional_labels == [] and r.human_attention is False
    assert r.signal_score == 0.9                                   # still stored
    assert r.reason == f"quarantined: {question} 0.40 reaches 0.40"


def test_below_quarantine_threshold_is_not_quarantined() -> None:
    r = gate.process(triage(safety={"possible_mnpi": 0.39, "instructs_ai": 0.39}), NO_REPEAT, T)
    assert r.gate == "stop" and r.decided_by == "jev"


def test_quarantine_reason_names_the_higher_question() -> None:
    r = gate.process(triage(safety={"possible_mnpi": 0.5, "instructs_ai": 0.8}), NO_REPEAT, T)
    assert r.reason == "quarantined: instructs_ai 0.80 reaches 0.40"

# thesis_relevant

## Definition
New, specific information that would support, challenge or change a reasonable investment thesis on a target company: its earnings expectations, competitive outlook, valuation or a trading decision.

## Rules
- R1: Choose this when the email reports a confirmed, specific fact such as a figure, guidance change, order, ruling or first-hand observation; early or unconfirmed signals belong in monitor.
- R2: The fact must be new rather than consensus; careful analysis that only restates widely known figures or guidance belongs in redundant.
- R3: Judge only the information itself, not sender prestige, writing quality, length or formatting; a short informal note with one material first-hand fact qualifies.
- R4: When one material item sits inside promotional or unrelated material, judge the email by that item and ignore the rest.
# monitor

## Definition
A credible emerging signal or developing trend that could affect a thesis on a target company, but whose implications remain uncertain or unconfirmed.

## Rules
- R1: Choose this for early, unconfirmed or partial evidence, such as a single channel check, a reported rumor or a pilot program, that bears on a target company.
- R2: The signal must have a plausible, stated path to affecting a target company's revenue, costs, competition or regulation; a weak or unsupported claim belongs in low_value.
- R3: Once the email gives a confirmed, specific figure or decision with clear effect on a target company
... [1240 more characters omitted]

def _floor4(x: float) -> float:
    scale = 10 ** limits.PASS_THRESHOLD_DECIMALS

[2026-10-09T04:27:07] ASSISTANT
Both requests are done, and all 225 tests pass.

**Monitor vs thesis-relevant boundary.** I rewrote the rules in three criteria files. The label definitions stay fixed, as the spec requires.
- **Thesis-relevant** now means a key actionable insight on one of the five named companies. Specific, quantified evidence counts even before the company confirms it, for example a first-hand channel check, panel or partner data with a figure, or a former insider's concrete account.
- **Monitor** is credible, relevant information worth tracking that isn't actionable on its own.
- **Low-value** takes unsourced rumors, teasers, hunches and data-vendor pitches.
- **Companies outside the five** go to irrelevant unless the email states the effect on one of them. The criteria never named the five companies before, and that was the biggest leak.

Measured with the criteria check on the tuning set:

| Measure | Fit, before → after | Validation, before → after |
|---|---|---|
| Signal accuracy | 55% → 87% | 76% → 94% |
| Exact-label accuracy | 40% → 69% | 58% → 82% |
| Gate recall | 100% → 100% | 100% → 100% |

The change fixed 20 emails but moved one morning-meeting roundup from thesis-relevant to monitor. So it doesn't strictly pass the spec's "breaks none" keep rule. I kept it because you asked for this boundary, and I recorded that in `DECISIONS.md` (#39).

**Thresholds in one place.** Every threshold, limit and tunable number is now defined once, in `src/triage_app/thresholds.py`, grouped by stage. Each value says whether it's fixed or tuned, and all code reads it as `thresholds.NAME`.
- **What moved in:** the values that used to live in `config.py`, plus constants that packages had defined locally, such as the split seed, chunking headroom, passage sizes, the monitor pass share and the model-call settings.
- **Tuned overrides:** `tuned/thresholds.json` still overrides the tuned values at run time, through `thresholds.load_thresholds()`.
- **Docs updated:** the spec, CLAUDE.md and the subagent rules now point at the new file.

  ### Files changed (all within my ownership)
  - `src/triage_app/state/apply.py` (new): pure functions over (seed, log).
    - `accept`, with edits for strength or new-pillar wording. An existing thesis logs `pillar_evidence`; a new thesis logs `pillar_added` with `next_pillar_id`.
    - `update_driver` fills `before` from the folded state and checks the driver's bounds. `set_conviction` takes 1 to 5.
    - `undo` appends a reversing entry for the last change still in effect.
    - `status_of`: "accepted" comes from the log, "dismissed" from the session set.
    - `conviction_reviews` returns a Suggestion with ID `<pillar>.review` when `fold.net_contradicting` reaches `thresholds.CONVICTION_REVIEW_STRENGTH`.

  - `src/triage_app/web/data.py`: loads the stage files from `TRIAGE_DATA_DIR` and reloads when a file's mtime changes. It also holds the quote highlighter and the quarantine filter for sections.
  - `src/triage_app/web/session.py`: per-session state in server memory, keyed by a signed cookie (Starlette `SessionMiddleware`), plus the rate limiter.
  - `src/triage_app/web/runner.py`: the live trace, "this mattered" and verify. Other packages' functions are imported lazily. Each step runs under `recorder.stage` on a fresh Recorder. A stage that raises `NotImplementedError` shows "not available yet". A failed stage shows only the exception's type.
  - `src/triage_app/web/templates/`: base, brief, suggestion, company, criteria, audit, inbox, email, live, eval, monitor and `fragments/*`. `src/triage_app/web/static/style.css`. htmx is pinned to `https://unpkg.com/htmx.org@2.0.4/dist/htmx.min.js`.

  The web tests cover these done-when points:
  - Accepting a thesis suggestion logs the evidence; accepting a new thesis adds AMZN.p4.
  - Updating MSFT.intelligent_cloud_growth to 26 changes EPS on `/company/MSFT` and in the returned fragment.
  - Reset restores the seed, undo reverses changes in order, and two clients get separate sessions.
  - Every GET route (all companies, criteria labels and emails) returns 200 with the banner. Unknown items return 404. A POST without HTMX gets a 303.
  - Conviction review: it is raised at the threshold, alerts first, set-conviction answers it, and an out-of-range value returns 422.
  - Verify, mattered and live are tested both as "not available yet" and with fake stage functions.
  - Live input cap (413), rate limit (429; presets are free; mattered shares the allowance; reset does not lift it).
  - Brief plus audit plus quarantined accounts for every fixture email exactly once.
  - Quarantined bodies (raw and parsed, plain and HTML-escaped) never appear in any response. That includes a quarantined pasted live email and a quarantined section injected into a suggestion.
  
  ### Run check
  `TRIAGE_DATA_DIR=tests/fixtures/out uv run uvicorn triage_app.web.main:app` serves the fixtures. I checked it in the browser: brief, accept by HTMX, assumption update recomputing EPS, error messages, and audit and monitor at phone width with no horizontal scroll.
  
  I also ran `run_live` once through the real stage functions with fake clients. parse, classify and gate ran. extract failed with `AdapterParseError` (expected: the FakeChat reply `{}` doesn't parse), so the later stages were skipped. redundancy failed with `ValueError` because the fixture `vectors.npy` has 8 dimensions and FakeEmbedder 64; the trace marks it "treated as no repeat". Real `day_1` vectors should not hit this.
  
  ### Lines for DECISIONS.md
  1. A suggestion's session status comes from the log (accepted) plus a session set (dismissed). Dismiss writes no log entry, so `/undo` does not reverse it.
  2. Undo reverses the last log entry still in effect and never targets an undo entry, so there is no redo.
  3. A conviction review has ID `<pillar_id>.review`. It stays open while the net contradicting strength is at least 4, until conviction is set against it (`conviction_changed` with that `suggestion_id`) or it is dismissed. Undoing the conviction change reopens it. Reviews come first in the alerts and count toward the three.
  4. Brief day is `SET_DATES[ctx.corpus_set]`, or the first parsed email's date when no set is given.
  5. An email whose suggestions fall in two lists appears on both cards ("that suggestion's card"). A new-thesis candidate whose emails are all monitor goes to worth_watching; stage 7 already rejects these.
  6. Attention alerts go only to non-quarantined emails flagged `human_attention` with Jev attention at or above `ALERT_ATTENTION`. A met "wrong if" test alerts on positions of at least `ALERT_WRONG_IF_MIN_BPS`, ordered by size.
  7. A pasted live email, "this mattered" and verify each spend one of the session's hourly runs. Presets do not, since they replay cached results. The run is refunded when the stage or agent isn't built. Reset keeps the run times, so it can't lift the limit.
  8. Live and mattered analysis runs against the visitor's book (seed plus session log), while validate and merge get the seed. Suggestions joining the session are re-IDed `<email_id>.<merged id>` so they can't collide with the day's IDs.
  9. A failed live step shows only the exception's type, never its message, so no email text (quarantined or not) can reach the page. A quarantined pasted email keeps only sender and subject in the session; its body is dropped.
  10. The suggestion page shows the current folded driver value as "book now", not the stored `book_value`, per "code fills the book's current value".
  11. Edits:
     - Strength on an all-monitor suggestion is capped at 1.
     - A driver update must be within bounds and different from the current value.
     - Conviction must be a whole number from 1 to 5.
  12. HTMX config swaps 4xx responses, and error messages are retargeted to `#flash`. A POST without HTMX redirects (303) back to the referer.
  13. The session cookie secret comes from `TRIAGE_SESSION_SECRET`, or is random per process when unset.
  
  ### For the lead (nothing left undone in my package)
  - **Live presets format:** each `data/live_presets/*.json` holds one `Email` or a list of them, and the preset's own `email_id` is kept so the cache hits.
  - **.env.example:** consider adding the optional `TRIAGE_DATA_DIR` and `TRIAGE_SESSION_SECRET`.
  - **REVIEW.md:** none; I generated no drafts.

  ### Other findings, most severe first
  
  **A. (High, functional) "This mattered" never analyses anything.**
  - `web/main.py:500` passes the stopped result to `runner.run_mattered`, which calls `pipeline/extract.process`. That function returns no claims whenever `result.gate != "pass"` (`pipeline/extract.py:73-74`).
  - Every "this mattered" click therefore spends one of the visitor's hourly runs, makes no model call and reports "no claims extracted / no suggestion".
  - I confirmed this with a scratch script: fixture_006 (stopped) gives 0 claims and 0 model calls.
  - `tests/test_web.py:332` monkeypatches `extract`, so it cannot catch this.
  - When this is fixed, add the quarantine guard noted in check 5 at the same time.
  
  **B. (Medium, design rule 2) Model-invented figures can pass validation.**
  - `ClaimDraft.value` and `period` are model output. Only `quote` is string-checked (`extract.py:78`).
  - Scenario: the quote says "Azure grew 31% y/y last quarter". The model writes `value=35, period="FY2027"`, and the skill proposes `stated_value=35`. Stage 7 keeps it (`validate.py:154-158`), and the card shows "Stated MSFT.intelligent_cloud_growth: 35" beside the book value.
  - A code check that the number appears in the claim's quote, and that the period is stated, would close it.
  
  **C. (Medium, robustness) One malformed model output aborts the whole stage, and the cache makes it permanent.**
  - No stage `run` loop catches per-email errors: `attention.py:28-32`, `extract.py:39-49`, `analyze.py:45-52`.
  - The raw completion is cached in `cached_call` before DSPy or Pydantic parses it. If the attention model returns, say, `action: "respond"` (outside the Literal), `notes.json` is never written for the set. Every rerun replays the same bad cached answer until that cache file is deleted.
  
  **D. (Medium) The Jev cache key omits the model ID.**
  - The payload is `{"state","questions"}` (`modules/classify.py:167`) plus the criteria version.
  - Changing `JEV_MODEL` (a new pin) silently replays the old model's answers, while `CallRecord.model` reports the new ID. This undermines the stated reason for the version pin.
  
  **E. (Medium) The live-route rate limit can be bypassed, and presets are not rate-limited.**
  - The limit is per cookie session (`session.py:37-48`, `main.py:348`). Dropping the cookie gives 5 fresh runs, so spend on the public URL is unbounded.
  - `SessionStore` never evicts sessions, so memory also grows without bound.
  - Presets skip the limit entirely (`main.py:336-339`). They are free only once their cache is warm.
  
  **F. (Medium, spec gaps in the deliverable)**
  - `data/live_presets/` holds 1 preset, not the six the spec asks for, including a quarantine case. That preset is a verbatim day_2 test email (`synthetic_000309`), not one written by the lead.
  - `tuned/` and `data/live_presets/` are untracked in git, so a clean clone runs at starting values and has no presets. `Dockerfile` `COPY tuned` fails in a clean clone.
  - DECISIONS #39 records that the kept criteria change broke a fit email, which violates the spec's keep rule; it was kept at your direction.
  
  **G. (Low) Live and "this mattered" validate against the seed, not the folded book.**
  - `runner.py:239` and `:254` pass `seed`, while analysis uses `fold(seed, log)` (`runner.py:225`).
  - Suggestions on a pillar the analyst accepted in this session (e.g. AAPL.p4) are rejected as an "unknown item".
  
  **H. (Low) The audit view omits the earlier email for some flagged repeats.**
  - `audit.html:17` shows the earlier email and both scores only when `redundant_of` is set, which happens only when the check changed the label.
  - Flagged repeats that Jev itself labeled redundant, or that passed, lose that detail. The spec asks for it on every flagged repeat.
  
  **I. (Low, docs)**
  - DECISIONS #21 still shows quarantine reasons with "reaches 0.40"; the threshold is 0.65 since #37.
  - CLAUDE.md says "Almost none of it is built yet".
  - `config.py:70` still says embeddings use fastembed.

  - `pass_signal` 0.98, and topic thresholds `macro` 0.07 and `other` 0.08, all fit on about 67 emails.
  - This follows the spec's rule, but expect a large gate reduction and many topic false positives on the test days.

    """Share of quoted sections, in notes and suggestions, found verbatim in the parsed body."""
    sections: list[tuple[str, LinkedSection]] = [(n.email_id, sec) for n in notes for sec in n.sections]
    sections += [(s.id, sec) for s in suggestions for sec in s.sections]
    misses = [Miss(email_id=sec.email_id, measure="quote_faithfulness", expected=f"verbatim quote in {owner}",
                   got=sec.quote[:80])
              for owner, sec in sections if not quote_in(sec.quote, bodies.get(sec.email_id, ""))]
    return Score(_ratio(len(sections) - len(misses), len(sections)), misses)

    - A quarantined email is refused outright: 403, no model call, no run spent. `run_mattered` also raises `ValueError` as a backstop.

    - A process-wide `GlobalRuns` cap is checked as well as the per-session cap. It covers pastes, "mattered", verify, and presets that are not yet cached.
    - A preset is marked warm after a run where every call was a cache hit. Warm presets are free, and that first all-cache-hit run is refunded.
    - A run is refunded when nothing ran, or when verify isn't available.
    - `SessionStore` is now least-recently-used: idle sessions past the TTL are evicted on access, and the store is capped at `MAX_SESSIONS`.
    - Tests: the global cap holds when cookies are cleared between visitors (pastes and verify), warm presets are free, and eviction works by both LRU and TTL.
  - **G. Live and mattered use the visitor's book.** They validate and merge against `runner.folded_seed(seed, log)`: theses and models from `fold(seed, log)`, links unchanged. The test shows AMZN.p4, accepted in the session, reaching both validate and merge.
  - **H. Audit shows every flagged repeat.** Any flagged record in `redundancy.json` shows its nearest email and both scores, with "Flagged as a possible repeat of" when there is no `redundant_of`. A quarantined nearest email shows only its sender and subject; the test checks no body leaks.

    - `MONITOR_TOP_EMAILS` now lives in `thresholds.py`.
    - `HTTPException` errors, including unknown paths, render `error.html` as HTML with the synthetic banner.
    - FastAPI's 422 form-validation errors still return JSON. They aren't `HTTPException`s, so the new handler doesn't catch them.

  ## Design choices
  - **Emails:** `RunContext(emails_path=...)` with a cached `ctx.emails` property that goes through `corpus.load`. It raises if no path is set, so a test can never fall back to a real corpus file by accident. `run_set` sets `config.corpus_file(set)`, and tests use `tests/fakes.FIXTURE_EMAILS`. `parse` is removed from `STAGES` completely (no load step). Every stage gets `out` as `in_dir`.
  - **Web:** `web/data.emails_file_for(dir)` maps `data/out/<set>` to `data/corpus/<set>/emails.jsonl`, and any other directory to the `emails.jsonl` beside it (that covers the fixtures). The `TRIAGE_EMAILS_FILE` env var overrides both, and `create_app(..., emails_path=)` is also available. `DayData.raw`/`parsed` became `inbox`. The email page now shows one "Body".
  - **Live day cache:** new `redundancy.build_day_cache(emails, ctx)` and `DayCache.copy()`. `runner.day_cache(data, ctx)` builds the cache once per loaded set and keeps it on `DayData`; each run gets its own copy. The first build's embedding calls show up in that run's redundancy step.
  - **RedundancyRecord:** I kept the contract shape. `process()` still returns the comparison for every email, which the live trace shows. `run()` writes only flagged records. Gate takes `RedundancyRecord | None`, and every reader uses `.get()`.
  - **Monitoring:** `Recorder.write` writes only `metrics.json`. I removed `thresholds.MONITOR_TOP_EMAILS` and the per-email lists on the Monitor page.
  - **Summary:** new `pipeline/summary.py`. `render` is pure and `read`/`write` handle the files. A quarantined email appears by ID only and other emails by subject. "Tuned" means the thresholds in force differ from `starting_thresholds()`.
  - **Docker:** `.dockerignore` re-includes `data/corpus/day_1/emails.jsonl` and `day_2/emails.jsonl` but not the plans or the tuning set. The Dockerfile has a comment explaining why.
  - **email_type:** `config.EMAIL_TYPES` holds the 11 types and `config.MEETING_LIKE_TYPES` is `{meeting_request, event_invitation, newsletter}`. Per your last message there is no mapping constant. The loader keeps the corpus `email_type` on a new optional `EmailLabel.email_type` field (evals only; it never reaches `Email`). The new metric `email_type_accuracy` counts quarantined emails as misses. Audit and email pages show `email_type`. `jev_questions.md` has 11 short descriptions by form and source.
  - **human_attention:**
    - Renamed `TriageRecord.human_attention`, `Thresholds.human_attention`, `HUMAN_ATTENTION` and `ALERT_HUMAN_ATTENTION`.
    - Renamed the eval keys to `human_attention_precision` and `human_attention_recall`.
    - **I renamed the module `pipeline/attention.py` → `pipeline/human_attention.py`** (and the test to `test_human_attention.py`). The stage label and the `metrics.json` key are now `human_attention`.
  - **Deliberately unchanged:**
    - `kind` fields with other meanings: `ClaimDraft.kind`, the suggestion bodies' `kind` discriminator, `Alert.kind`, the review-sheet `kind` column, and `skills.*.kind`.
    - Names that are not the probability field: `Brief.needs_attention`, `NoteDraft.why_attention`, the `AttentionNote` contract, `notes.json`, and `modules/attention.py` (including its cache namespace `"attention"`, so cached calls stay valid).
    - `scripts/jev_spike.py`, a historical spike script.
  - **Rename script:** I made one script, `scripts/rename_output_keys.py`, and checked it is idempotent. It rewrites `triage.json` (`kind`, `kind_probs`, `attention`), `eval.json` and `criteria_history.json` measure keys, the `metrics.json` stage key, and `tuned/thresholds.json`. I ran it on the fixtures and on `tuned/thresholds.json`, which is committed because the code now needs the new key. **I did not commit any `data/out` rewrites.**
  - **Fixtures:**
    - Deleted `raw.json`, `parsed.json`, `vectors.npy` and `usage.json`. `redundancy.json` now holds only fixture_006.
    - `triage.json` uses 11-type probabilities. Jev's type matches the corpus type except on fixture_006, so `email_type_accuracy` is 0.7.
    - `emails.jsonl` and `labels.jsonl` carry 11-type `email_type` values.
    - Removed the `parse` stage from `metrics.json` and regenerated `eval.json` with the code.
    - Added `SUMMARY.md`, produced by the function.
    - The validator no longer uses vectors. It checks bodies through the loader, that `redundancy.json` is flagged-only, that `metrics.json` is internally consistent, that `SUMMARY.md` equals `render()`, and that it leaks no body or reason.
  - **New tests:** `tests/test_summary.py` (fixture summary, no body or label, tuned vs starting, partial run, `run_set` writing only its outputs) and web tests for the day cache, the emails-file mapping and the `email_type` display.
  - **One extra test fix:** `test_criteria_check.py` now sets `JEV_MODEL` itself with `monkeypatch`. Before, it depended on `.env`, so it fails in a checkout without one (like this worktree).

def _figure_in_bounds(a: LinkedAssumption, pillar: Pillar | None, model: CompanyModel | None) -> bool:
    if pillar is None or model is None or a.driver_id not in pillar.driver_ids:
        return False
    driver = next((d for d in model.drivers if d.id == a.driver_id), None)
    return driver is not None and driver.min <= a.stated_value <= driver.max


def _figure_in_period(a: LinkedAssumption, claims: list[Claim], model: CompanyModel | None) -> bool:
    if model is None:
        return False
    return any(c.value is not None and math.isclose(c.value, a.stated_value) and c.period == model.fiscal_year
               for c in claims)

class NoteFields(BaseModel):          # what a note says; shared by the draft and the stored note
    summary: str                        # two sentences
    why_attention: str                  # one or two sentences
    action: Literal["reply", "attend", "decide", "read", "other"]
    deadline: datetime | None = None    # only when the email states one

class NoteDraft(NoteFields):           # stage A model output
    sections: list[QuoteDraft]

class ClaimDraft(BaseModel):            # stage 5 model output, one per claim
    quote: str                          # verbatim section of the email
    tickers: list[Ticker]               # the covered companies the claim bears on
    entities: list[str]                 # companies outside coverage
    kind: Literal["reported_fact", "guidance", "estimate_change",
                  "rating_change", "channel_check", "management_comment",
                  "opinion"]
    metric: str | None = None
    period: str | None = None           # e.g. "FY2027"
    value: float | None = None
    unit: Literal["pct", "usd_bn", "usd", "multiple"] | None = None

def earlier_context(record: RedundancyRecord | None, emails: dict[str, Email],
                    results: dict[str, EmailResult]) -> Email | None:
    """The nearest earlier email when this one is a flagged repeat, unless that one was quarantined."""
    if record is None or not record.flagged or record.nearest is None:
        return None
    nearest = results.get(record.nearest)
    for result in results:  # results.json is in arrival order
        if result.gate != "pass":
            continue

2. Quote mismatch (reject): a section not found verbatim in the email body (`quote_in`).
3. Duplicate thesis (reject): a new-thesis statement whose cosine with any existing
   pillar statement reaches `thresholds.NEW_THESIS_DUPLICATE` (stage 2 embedding model).

def _figure_in_period(a: LinkedAssumption, claims: list[Claim], model: CompanyModel | None) -> bool:
    if model is None:
        return False
    return any(c.value is not None and math.isclose(c.value, a.stated_value) and c.period == model.fiscal_year
               for c in claims)

[2026-10-09T05:44:58] TOOL RESULT
def process(email_id: str, claims: list[Claim], result: EmailResult, ctx: RunContext,
            *, book: BookState | None = None,
            agent: AnalysisAgent | None = None) -> tuple[AnalysisRecord, list[Suggestion]]:
    """Run the analysis agent (at most three skill calls) for one email."""
    if not claims:
        return AnalysisRecord(email_id=email_id, skills_called=[], suggestion_ids=[],
                              no_change_reason=NO_CLAIMS), []
    book = book or fold(load_seed(), [])
    context = SkillContext(book=book, candidate_pillar_ids=candidate_pillars(claims, book),
                           email_triage={email_id: result.triage or "unlabeled"})
    outcome = (agent or AnalysisAgent(ctx.chat))(claims, context)
    suggestions = collect(email_id, outcome, book)
    return AnalysisRecord(
        email_id=email_id,
        skills_called=outcome.skills_called,
        suggestion_ids=[s.id for s in suggestions],
        no_change_reason=None if suggestions else no_change_reason(outcome),
    ), suggestions

def accept(seed: Seed, log: list[LogEntry], suggestion: Suggestion, *, strength: Strength | None = None,
           statement: str | None = None, wrong_if: str | None = None, at: datetime | None = None) -> LogEntry:
    """Accept one suggestion, optionally edited: a new strength, or new wording for a new pillar."""
    if suggestion.status == "rejected":

    def forward(self, email: Email) -> NoteDraft:
        with dspy.context(adapter=json_adapter()):
            out = self.predict(sender=email.sender, sender_email=email.sender_email,
                               subject=email.subject, received_at=email.received_at.isoformat(),
                               body=delimit_body(email.body), lm=self.lm)

    def forward(self, email: Email, earlier: Email | None = None) -> list[ClaimDraft]:

[2026-10-09T07:14:21] ASSISTANT
The notebook is done, and so are the cleanup and the remaining steps. Everything is committed, the working tree is clean, all 260 tests pass, and strict mypy passes on `src` and `tests` with zero `type: ignore` comments.

**The notebook** is `notebooks/walkthrough.ipynb`, saved with its outputs. It runs the real stage functions one per cell, with an explanation before each, on three `day_1` emails:

1. **Thesis-relevant, then a thesis edit (`synthetic_000261`):** an informal note from a display supplier saying iPhone Pro Max panel orders were raised 9–12%.
   - Jev labels it thesis-relevant (0.81).
   - Extraction produces claims with verbatim quotes, and the agent calls the existing-thesis skill. It suggests the email contradicts AAPL.p1, the short "iPhone cycle disappoints" pillar.
   - Validation keeps the suggestion. The analyst accepts it and the evidence is logged against the pillar.
2. **Human attention (`synthetic_000144`):** an expert-call invitation that Jev flags for a person (0.96). The attention note gives a summary, why it needs a person, the action (reply) and the deadline.
3. **Irrelevant (`synthetic_000007`):** strong MedTech research with nothing on the five companies. Jev labels it irrelevant (0.91), the gate stops it with a one-line reason, and no further model sees it.

Each case ends with the corpus label for comparison; no stage sends labels to a model. The notebook replays entirely from the cache, so rerunning it costs 0 tokens. The rebuild command is in the README.

**What else got done:**
- **Cheapest models:** both generative roles use Claude Haiku 5.5. At its price, a 300-email day costs roughly $0.20–0.30 in model calls; that's my estimate from the token counts.
- **Haiku's reply formats:** Haiku often returns JSON in shapes DSPy can't parse. Two fixes:
  - **Lenient parser:** handles a code fence, a bare list, DSPy's chat-adapter format, and Haiku's own tool-call markup.
  - **Retries:** a reply that still can't be parsed gets up to two fresh calls instead of replaying the bad cached one.

  Both days now finish with no failed emails at any stage. Before this, 48 of 66 extractions on `day_1` had failed.
- **Review fixes:**
  - **Stated figures:** a figure must literally appear in its claim's quote.
  - **Robustness:** one bad reply no longer aborts a stage.
  - **Jev cache key:** it now includes the model.

**Final scores on the test days** (four targets missed; every miss is listed in each day's `eval.json`):

| Measure | `day_1` | `day_2` | Target |
|---|---|---|---|
| Gate recall | 86% | 67% | 100% (missed) |
| Monitor recall at the gate | 85% | 92% | 95% (missed) |
| Signal accuracy | 84.7% | 84.7% | 85% (just missed) |
| Ticker F1 | 0.77 | 0.83 | 0.90 (missed) |
| Human attention precision / recall | 85% / 89.5% | 82% / 90% | 80% / 90% |
| Quote faithfulness | 100% | 100% | 100% |
| Stray suggestions | 0 | 0 | reported |

**Decisions for you:**
- **Gate threshold:** the misses mostly come from the spec's tuning rule, which set the pass threshold to 0.98 with zero safety margin. Adding a margin rule, chosen on the tuning set and not by looking at these test scores, would likely recover most of the recall. Want me to make that change?
- **Repeat detection:** the fixed redundancy threshold flags too many emails that are merely on the same topic (precision 7–10%).

        return 0.0
    xs = sorted(values)
    pos = (len(xs) - 1) * q / 100
    lo = int(pos)
    hi = min(lo + 1, len(xs) - 1)
    return xs[lo] + (xs[hi] - xs[lo]) * (pos - lo)


def _usage(calls: list[CallRecord], latencies: list[float]) -> StageUsage:
    misses = [c for c in calls if not c.cache_hit]
    return StageUsage(
        calls=len(calls),
        cache_hits=len(calls) - len(misses),
        input_tokens=[REDACTED] for c in misses),
        output_tokens=[REDACTED] for c in misses),
        uncached_input_tokens=[REDACTED] for c in calls),
        uncached_output_tokens=[REDACTED] for c in calls),
        latency_p50_ms=percentile(latencies, 50),
        latency_p95_ms=percentile(latencies, 95),
        latency_max_ms=max(latencies, default=0.0),
    )


def build_report(corpus: ReportCorpus, calls: list[CallRecord], timings: list[StageTiming],
                 emails: int) -> UsageReport:
    """Stage latency comes from per-email StageTimings; model latency from CallRecords."""
    calls_by_stage: dict[str, list[CallRecord]] = defaultdict(list)
    for c in calls:

  ## Prompt and tool rewrite: final report
  
  All six requested changes are made to the prompt, skill and tool files. The check script passes, the tests pass, and `mypy src` is clean. `mypy tests` has one error, in a file the projection-code session owns. I did not edit any code, call any model or commit.
  
  ### Files changed (all under `/Users/kelvinwallace/Desktop/DE_Shaw_CaseStudy/.claude/worktrees/analysis-upgrade/`)
  - `instructions/attention_note.md`:
    - The `summary` now opens with the target stock(s) and the opportunity on offer.
    - `why_attention` now says what attending or replying gives the desk.
    - New `follow_up` field: who on the desk does what, by when. It names a role, never an invented person, and never a trade.
    - `action` and `deadline` are unchanged. Quotes come from `body` only.
    - The JSON shape includes `follow_up`, and the notes are written in the third person.
    - There are now 3 examples: an NVDA expert call (attend), an MSFT CFO breakfast (reply, deadline is the end of a date-only day), and a sales pitch (other).
  - `instructions/extract_claims.md`:
    - New "Stated projections" section. Every stated EPS, revenue, operating income, margin, segment growth, capex or target price becomes its own claim with `metric`, `period`, `value` and `unit` filled.
    - It covers unit mapping, level versus change, and one claim per figure when a sentence states several.
    - New Example 2 is an NVDA model update. It shows a target price with no period left as `period: null`.
  - `skills/alter_existing_thesis.md`:
    - A suggestion now needs a direct match to the pillar's exact statement or its "wrong if" test.
    - Each suggestion carries `relevance`, with calibration bands (0.9–1, 0.7–0.89, 0.4–0.69, below 0.4) and an example for each. Code rejects anything below 0.7 and keeps at most the 2 most relevant.
    - `"stance": "none"` is now the expected outcome when the match isn't exact. It takes strength 1 and an empty street note, and `no_change_reason` is filled whenever no suggestion supports or contradicts.
    - The desk's stance is compared with the street's view. `street_view_shift` is set only for a sell-side `rating_change` or `estimate_change`. `street_view_note` is required on every kept suggestion.
    - There are 4 examples: a direct match, a sell-side downgrade moving the street toward sell, a loose match returned as none, and a borderline case with two pillars where only one is kept.
  - `skills/spawn_new_thesis.md`: the `rationale` is now 3–5 sentences in a fixed order (what was noticed, why no pillar covers it, what must be true, what would prove it wrong). The examples and no-change reasons are reworded so none mentions skills.
  - `skills/revise_projections.md` (new, laid out like the other skills):
    - It uses the inputs `projection_code` already built in `modules/skills.py`: `ticker`, `claims`, `email_triage`, `companies` (fiscal_year, drivers, projections).
    - Only stated figures for the exact modeled fiscal year count. `metric` is a listed driver ID or one of revenue / operating_income / eps / target_price, and the units must match.
    - It never computes, converts or derives a figure. A change-only claim is not a projection. Each distinct stated figure gets its own suggestion.
    - The rationale says above, below or in line with the desk and consensus, without repeating the book's values.
    - There are 3 examples: EPS plus a driver, with the target price dropped for having no period; a wrong year; and a company-wide growth rate that fits no driver.
  - `tools/analysis_agent.json`:
    - Third tool `revise_projections` added, with `claim_ids` and `ticker` and a "when not to call it" line.

def accepted_ids(log: list[LogEntry]) -> set[str]:
    """Suggestion IDs with an accept still in effect."""
    return {e.suggestion_id for e in effective_entries(log)
            if e.change in ("pillar_evidence", "pillar_added")}

usage: python3 -m triage_app.evals.criteria_check [-h] [--criteria CRITERIA]

Criteria check: rerun Jev on the tuning set with the current criteria files and compare.

    uv run python -m triage_app.evals.criteria_check

Reruns stage 3 (classify, through ctx.jev and the disk cache) and stage 4 (gate) on every
tuning email, refits the thresholds in memory on the fit split (never written to
tuned/thresholds.json), and prints the label measures before and after on the fit and
validation splits, with every email whose label changed. "Before" is the previous
criteria version's entry in data/out/criteria_history.json. The check then appends this
version's entry there (one entry per version: rerunning a version replaces its entry).

Each version's labels are kept beside the tuning output, in
data/out/tuning/criteria_check/<version>.json (EmailResult list), so the next version can
list the emails it relabeled. Nothing else is written. See SPEC.md, "Relevance criteria"
and the keep rule under "Tuning".

options:
  -h, --help           show this help message and exit
  --criteria CRITERIA  criteria directory

[2026-10-09T08:57:58] ASSISTANT
I tested six rule changes for Jev on the tuning set only (fit split to choose, validation split to check), never on the test days. I recommend adopting two of them, but they miss the spec's keep rule by one email, so I need your call before changing the criteria files.

**The two recommended rules:**
- **`monitor.md`, new R7:** a teaser, free sample, trial offer or event invitation that only hints at a covered company is not monitor; it belongs in low-value because it brings no new evidence of its own. This targets the biggest error that affects the gate: data-vendor samples and rumor panels about Microsoft or Amazon labeled monitor.
- **`thesis_relevant.md`, new R7:** a figure reported as steady, unchanged or in line with a widely tracked level adds nothing new, so it's redundant, not thesis-relevant. This targets cases like "Amazon parcel share steady at about 28%".

**Results with both rules:**

| Measure | Fit | Validation |
|---|---|---|
| Exact-label accuracy | 0.687 → 0.716 | 0.818 → 0.788 |
| Signal accuracy | 0.866 → 0.896 | 0.939 → 0.939 (unchanged) |

They fix 3 fit emails and break 1. The keep rule allows zero breaks, so strictly this fails. The broken email, and the one validation drop, are data-vendor pitches flipping between low-value and irrelevant. The corpus labels those pitches about evenly both ways (7 low-value and 6 irrelevant in the tuning set), so they're closer to label noise than a rule failure. Neither flip changes which emails pass the gate.

**What didn't work:**
- Loosening the "outside companies are irrelevant" rule let tobacco research back in as thesis-relevant, and signal accuracy fell to 0.821.
- The monitor rule alone made teasers slide to irrelevant rather than low-value.
- A rule aimed squarely at vendor pitches just moved the noise onto other emails.

Every trial version is recorded in `data/out/criteria_history.json`. The real criteria files are unchanged so far.

**The Sonnet rerun mostly failed.** Extraction failed on 51 of 64 passed emails on `day_1` and 60 of 62 on `day_2`, because OpenRouter calls kept erroring, most likely sustained rate limiting. That left the brief with no suggestions, so the scores I pulled from that run reflect classification only. A single Sonnet call works now. I've made failures log the provider's status line, and a retry run on both days from the notes stage is going now; successes are cached, so only the failed emails cost anything.

Should I adopt the two rules despite the one-email miss? If you say yes, I'll rerun Jev on both test days with the new rules, rerun the analysis for any email whose gate decision changes, then score.

    def _post(self, body: dict[str, Any]) -> Completion:
        if not self._api_key:
            raise RuntimeError("OPENROUTER_API_KEY is not set")
        for attempt in range(thresholds.LLM_MAX_RETRIES + 1):
            _throttle(body["model"])
            resp = httpx.post(
                f"{self.base_url}/chat/completions",
                headers={"Authorization": f"Bearer {self._api_key}", "Content-Type": "application/json"},
                json=body, timeout=self.timeout,
            )
            retryable = resp.status_code == 429 or resp.status_code >= 500
            if retryable and attempt < thresholds.LLM_MAX_RETRIES:
                time.sleep(_retry_delay(resp, attempt))
                continue
            break
        if resp.status_code >= 400:
            raise RuntimeError(f"OpenRouter {resp.status_code}: {resp.text[:500]}")

def process(triage: TriageRecord, redundancy: RedundancyRecord | None, thresholds: Thresholds) -> EmailResult:
    """Quarantine, label, tickers and topics, attention, gate; reason composed by code.

    `redundancy` is the email's flagged record, or None when the check did not flag it.
    """
    signal = signal_score(triage)

    # 1. Quarantine on either safety question; nothing further runs.
    tripped = {q: p for q, p in triage.safety.items() if p >= limits.QUARANTINE}
    if tripped:
        question = max(tripped, key=lambda q: tripped[q])
        return EmailResult(
            email_id=triage.email_id, triage=None, decided_by="quarantine", redundant_of=None,
            affected_tickers=[], additional_labels=[], human_attention=False,
            signal_score=signal, gate="quarantine",
            reason=f"quarantined: {question} {tripped[question]:.2f} reaches {limits.QUARANTINE:.2f}",
        )

    # 2. Label: Jev's most probable label, unless the check flags a repeat below the gate.
    jev_label = max(config.TRIAGE_LABELS, key=lambda label: triage.triage_probs.get(label, 0.0))
    jev_part = f"{jev_label} {triage.triage_probs.get(jev_label, 0.0):.2f}"
    label, decided_by, redundant_of, label_part = jev_label, "jev", None, jev_part
    if (redundancy is not None and redundancy.flagged and signal < thresholds.pass_signal
            and jev_label != "redundant" and redundancy.nearest is not None):
        label, decided_by, redundant_of = "redundant", "redunda

def _figure_stated(a: LinkedAssumption, claims: list[Claim], model: CompanyModel | None) -> bool:
    if model is None:
        return False
    return any(c.value is not None and math.isclose(c.value, a.stated_value) and c.period == model.fiscal_year
               and any(math.isclose(n, abs(a.stated_value)) for n in numbers_in(c.quote))
               for c in claims)


def _check_relevance(body: ExistingThesis) -> None:
    if body.relevance < thresholds.MIN_PILLAR_RELEVANCE:
        raise Rejected(f"weak match: relevance {body.relevance:g} below {thresholds.MIN_PILLAR_RELEVANCE:g}")


def _check_projection(body: ProjectionChange, claims: list[Claim], model: CompanyModel) -> None:
    if body.period != model.fiscal_year:
        raise Rejected(f"wrong period: {body.period} is not {body.ticker}'s modeled year {model.fiscal_year}")

def check(suggestion: Suggestion, ctx: RunContext, inputs: ValidationInputs) -> Suggestion:
    """Apply rules 1 to 9 in order; raises Rejected at the first rule that rejects."""
    email_id = email_of(suggestion.id)
    _check_unknown(suggestion, email_id, inputs)
    _check_quotes(suggestion, inputs)
    body = suggestion.body
    if isinstance(body, ExistingThesis):
        _check_relevance(body)
    if isinstance(body, NewThesis):
        _check_duplicate(body, inputs, ctx)
    if isinstance(body, ProjectionChange):
        _check_projection(body, [inputs.claims[c] for c in suggestion.claim_ids], inputs.models[body.ticker])
    monitor_only = all(_label(inputs.results, e) == "monitor" for e in linked_emails(suggestion))
    if isinstance(body, NewThesis):
        if monitor_only:
            raise Rejected("monitor only: a new thesis needs an email not labeled monitor")

        pillar = inputs.pillars.get(body.pillar_id)
        model = inputs.models.get(body.pillar_id.split(".")[0])
        claims = [inputs.claims[c] for c in suggestion.claim_ids]
        kept = [a for a in body.assumptions
                if _figure_in_bounds(a, pillar, model) and _figure_stated(a, claims, model)]

    checked = suggestion.mod

class DiskCache:
    """One JSON file per entry: {"value": ..., "input_tokens": n, "output_tokens": n, "estimated": b}."""

    def __init__(self, root: Path = CACHE_DIR) -> None:
        self.root = root

    def _path(self, namespace: str, key: str) -> Path:
        return self.root / namespace / key[:2] / f"{key}.json"

    def get(self, namespace: str, key: str) -> dict[str, Any] | None:
        path = self._path(namespace, key)
        if not path.exists():
            return None
        entry: dict[str, Any] = json.loads(path.read_text())
        return entry

    def put(self, namespace: str, key: str, entry: dict[str, Any]) -> None:
        path = self._path(namespace, key)
        path.parent.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
        path.write_text(json.dumps(entry, ensure_ascii=False, sort_keys=True))
